The transcript has grown since the last condensation, AND\n//  2. The new transcript portion contains file modifications, AND\n//  3. At least one modified file overlaps with the currently staged files\n//\n// The overlap check ensures we don't add checkpoint trailers to commits that are\n// unrelated to the agent's recent changes.\n//\n// This handles the scenario where the agent commits mid-session before Stop.\nfunc (s *ManualCommitStrategy) sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript(repo *git.Repository, state *SessionState) (bool, error) {\n\tlogCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), \"checkpoint\")\n\n\t// Need both transcript path and agent type to analyze\n\tif state.TranscriptPath == \"\" || state.AgentType == \"\" {\n\t\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"live transcript check: missing transcript path or agent type\",\n\t\t\tslog.String(\"session_id\", state.SessionID),\n\t\t\tslog.String(\"transcript_path\", state.TranscriptPath),\n\t\t\tslog.String(\"agent_type\", string(state.AgentType)),\n\t\t)\n\t\treturn false, nil\n\t}\n\n\t// Get the agent for transcript analysis\n\tag, err := agent.GetByAgentType(state.AgentType)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn false, nil //nolint:nilerr // Unknown agent type, fail gracefully\n\t}\n\n\t// Cast to TranscriptAnalyzer\n\tanalyzer, ok := ag.(agent.TranscriptAnalyzer)\n\tif !ok {\n\t\treturn false, nil // Agent doesn't support transcript analysis\n\t}\n\n\t// Get current transcript position\n\tcurrentPos, err := analyzer.GetTranscriptPosition(state.TranscriptPath)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn false, nil //nolint:nilerr // Error reading transcript, fail gracefully\n\t}\n\n\t// Check if transcript has grown since last condensation\n\tif currentPos <= state.CheckpointTranscriptStart {\n\t\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"live transcript check: no new content\",\n\t\t\tslog.String(\"session_id\", state.SessionID),\n\t\t\tslog.Int(\"current_pos\", currentPos),\n\t\t\tslog.Int(\"start_offset\", state.CheckpointTranscriptStart),\n\t\t)\n\t\treturn false, nil // No new content\n\t}\n\n\t// Transcript has grown - check if there are file modifications in the new portion\n\tmodifiedFiles, _, err := analyzer.ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset(state.TranscriptPath, state.CheckpointTranscriptStart)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn false, nil //nolint:nilerr // Error parsing transcript, fail gracefully\n\t}\n\n\t// No file modifications means no new content to checkpoint\n\tif len(modifiedFiles) == 0 {\n\t\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"live transcript check: transcript grew but no file modifications\",\n\t\t\tslog.String(\"session_id\", state.SessionID),\n\t\t)\n\t\treturn false, nil\n\t}\n\n\t// Normalize modified files from absolute to repo-relative paths.\n\t// Transcript tool_use entries contain absolute paths (e.g., /Users/alex/project/src/main.go)\n\t// but getStagedFiles returns repo-relative paths (e.g., src/main.go).\n\tworktreePath, wpErr := GetWorktreePath()\n\tif wpErr == nil {\n\t\tnormalized := make([]string, 0, len(modifiedFiles))\n\t\tfor _, f := range modifiedFiles {\n\t\t\tif rel := paths.ToRelativePath(f, worktreePath); rel != \"\" {\n\t\t\t\tnormalized = append(normalized, rel)\n\t\t\t} else {\n\t\t\t\t// Already relative or outside repo — keep as-is\n\t\t\t\tnormalized = append(normalized, f)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t\tmodifiedFiles = normalized\n\t}\n\n\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"live transcript check: found file modifications\",\n\t\tslog.String(\"session_id\", state.SessionID),\n\t\tslog.Int(\"modified_files\", len(modifiedFiles)),\n\t)\n\n\t// Check if any modified files overlap with currently staged files\n\t// This ensures we only add checkpoint trailers to commits that include\n\t// files the agent actually modified\n\tstagedFiles := getStagedFiles(repo)\n\n\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"live transcript check: comparing staged vs modified\",\n\t\tslog.String(\"session_id\", state.SessionID),\n\t\tslog.Int(\"staged_files\", len(stagedFiles)),\n\t\tslog.Int(\"modified_files\", len(modifiedFiles)),\n\t)\n\n\tif !hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, modifiedFiles) {\n\t\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"live transcript check: no overlap between staged and modified files\",\n\t\t\tslog.String(\"session_id\", state.SessionID),\n\t\t)\n\t\treturn false, nil // No overlap - staged files are unrelated to agent's work\n\t}\n\n\treturn true, nil\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/alex/workspace/cli/.worktrees/1/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go",
  "old_string": "// filterSessionsWithNewContent returns sessions that have new transcript content\n// beyond what was already condensed.",
  "new_string": "// postCommitUpdateBaseCommitOnly updates BaseCommit for all sessions on the current\n// worktree when a commit has no Entire-Checkpoint trailer. This prevents BaseCommit\n// from going stale, which would cause future PrepareCommitMsg calls to skip the\n// session (BaseCommit != currentHeadHash filter).\n//\n// Unlike the full PostCommit flow, this does NOT fire EventGitCommit or trigger\n// condensation — it only keeps BaseCommit in sync with HEAD.\nfunc (s *ManualCommitStrategy) postCommitUpdateBaseCommitOnly(logCtx context.Context, head *plumbing.Reference) {\n\tworktreePath, err := GetWorktreePath()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn // Silent failure — hooks must be resilient\n\t}\n\n\tsessions, err := s.findSessionsForWorktree(worktreePath)\n\tif err != nil || len(sessions) == 0 {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tnewHead := head.Hash().String()\n\tfor _, state := range sessions {\n\t\tif state.BaseCommit != newHead {\n\t\t\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"post-commit (no trailer): updating BaseCommit\",\n\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"session_id\", state.SessionID),\n\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"old_base\", state.BaseCommit[:7]),\n\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"new_head\", newHead[:7]),\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t\tstate.BaseCommit = newHead\n\t\t\tif err := s.saveSessionState(state); err != nil {\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"[entire] Warning: failed to update session state: %v\\n\", err)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\n// filterSessionsWithNewContent returns sessions that have new transcript content\n// beyond what was already condensed."
}

    26→// Scanner buffer size for large transcript files (10MB)
    27→const scannerBufferSize = 10 * 1024 * 1024

    29→// ParseTranscript parses raw JSONL content into transcript lines
    30→func ParseTranscript(data []byte) ([]TranscriptLine, error) {
    31→	var lines []TranscriptLine
    32→	scanner := bufio.NewScanner(bytes.NewReader(data))
    33→	scanner.Buffer(make([]byte, 0, scannerBufferSize), scannerBufferSize)
    34→
    35→	for scanner.Scan() {
    36→		var line TranscriptLine
    37→		if err := json.Unmarshal(scanner.Bytes(), &line); err != nil {
    38→			continue // Skip malformed lines
    39→		}
    40→		lines = append(lines, line)
    41→	}
    42→
    43→	if err := scanner.Err(); err != nil {
    44→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to scan transcript: %w", err)
    45→	}
    46→	return lines, nil
    47→}
    48→
    49→// SerializeTranscript converts transcript lines back to JSONL bytes
    50→func SerializeTranscript(lines []TranscriptLine) ([]byte, error) {
    51→	var buf bytes.Buffer
    52→	for _, line := range lines {
    53→		data, err := json.Marshal(line)
    54→		if err != nil {
    55→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal line: %w", err)
    56→		}
    57→		buf.Write(data)
    58→		buf.WriteByte('\n')
    59→	}
    60→	return buf.Bytes(), nil
    61→}

> AGENT

I see the issue. The transcript format for an assistant message needs the `message` field to be a raw JSON object that parses as an `AssistantMessage` with `Content` array containing `ContentBlock`s. The `Input` is a `json.RawMessage` which must parse to a `ToolInput` struct. My test transcript's message field is not properly formatted — it needs to wrap `content` in a `message` field as `json.RawMessage`.

Let me look at the exact format in the JSONL file. The `Line` struct has `type` and `message` (raw JSON). The message for an assistant type must be an `AssistantMessage` with a `content` array of `ContentBlock`s. Each `ContentBlock` with `type: "tool_use"` has a `name` and `input` (which is `json.RawMessage` that maps to `ToolInput`).

So the correct transcript line for an assistant Write tool_use would be:

```json
{"type":"assistant","message":{"content":[{"type":"tool_use","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/abs/path/file.go","content":"..."}}]}}
```

`GetTranscriptPosition` counts raw lines, so 2 lines (human + assistant) with `startOffset=0` means `currentPos=2 > 0`. Good.

`ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset` reads lines, skips lines with `lineNum <= startOffset`, and for lines after the offset, it parses them as `TranscriptLine`. It only processes lines of type "assistant". In my test, the line is `"type":"human"` and `"type":"assistant"` — but the human line uses `"type":"human"` which is not "user". The parser should still correctly extract the assistant message.

The issue is that `getStagedFiles` in the test context might not work as expected. In the test, we do `wt.Add("src/main.go")` which stages the file. But `sessionHasNewContent` first falls through to the live transcript check because there's no shadow branch. The shadow branch doesn't exist because we never called `SaveChanges`.

1. Checks transcript path and agent type ✓

3. Gets transcript position (2 lines) ✓
4. Checks if position > startOffset (2 > 0 = true) ✓
5. ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset — this should return the absolute file path ✓
6. Normalizes paths (the bug fix) ✓
7. Gets staged files ✓
8. Checks overlap ✓

But wait — in the test, `wt.Add("src/main.go")` stages the file. But then we call `sessionHasNewContent` directly, without committing. So `getStagedFiles` should return `["src/main.go"]`. But there might be a subtlety — the file was just created but maybe never committed before, so it's "untracked" not "staged".

Let me check what `getStagedFiles` does — it checks `fileStatus.Staging != git.Unmodified && fileStatus.Staging != git.Untracked`. After `wt.Add`, the staging status should be `git.Added`. That should work.

> AGENT

There it is! The classic macOS symlink issue:
- `dir` = `/var/folders/...` (from `t.TempDir()`)
- `worktreePath` = `/private/var/folders/...` (from `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`, which resolves symlinks)
- `absFilePath` = `/var/folders/...` (from `filepath.Join(dir, ...)`)

`paths.ToRelativePath` uses `filepath.Rel()` which fails because `/var/...` vs `/private/var/...` don't have a common prefix.

The fix in the production code is correct (using `GetWorktreePath()` which returns the resolved path). The test just needs to use the resolved path too when constructing the absolute file path in the transcript. Let me fix the test:

> TOOL

id: toolu_01QssgZXZf2vu9x66nmzZUED

Claude Code uses the resolved\n\t// path in its transcript, so we must too.\n\tworktreePath, err := GetWorktreePath()\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\tworktreeID, err := paths.GetWorktreeID(worktreePath)\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\n\t// Create a transcript file that references the file by absolute path\n\t// (this is what Claude Code does — tool_use Write has absolute file_path)\n\tabsFilePath := filepath.Join(worktreePath, \"src\", \"main.go\")\n\ttranscriptContent := `{\"type\":\"human\",\"message\":{\"content\":\"write a main.go file\"}}\n{\"type\":\"assistant\",\"message\":{\"content\":[{\"type\":\"tool_use\",\"name\":\"Write\",\"input\":{\"file_path\":\"` + absFilePath + `\",\"content\":\"package main\\n\"}}]}}\n`\n\ttranscriptPath := filepath.Join(dir, \"transcript.jsonl\")\n\trequire.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(transcriptPath, []byte(transcriptContent), 0o644))\n\n\t// Create session state: no shadow branch (it was deleted after last condensation),\n\t// transcript path points to the file, agent type is Claude Code\n\tnow := time.Now()"
}

> AGENT

The test expects that the second commit has a DIFFERENT checkpoint ID from the first. But both get `af924955f841`. The problem is that after the first commit + condensation, `LastCheckpointID` is set and `PendingCheckpointID` is also set. Now with my fix (Bug 3), `SaveChanges` no longer clears `PendingCheckpointID`. So during the second commit's `PrepareCommitMsg`, the `PendingCheckpointID` from the first commit is still present and gets reused.

Let me look at the flow more carefully:

1. First commit: PrepareCommitMsg generates `af924955f841`, PostCommit condenses, sets `PendingCheckpointID = "af924955f841"` and `LastCheckpointID = "af924955f841"`.
2. Phase 3: No `SimulateUserPromptSubmit` call (no InitializeSession) — so `PendingCheckpointID` and `LastCheckpointID` are NOT cleared.
3. Agent does work, Stop hook fires, SaveChanges runs. Before my fix, SaveChanges would clear `PendingCheckpointID`. Now it doesn't.
4. Second commit: PrepareCommitMsg checks `PendingCheckpointID` — it finds `"af924955f841"` and reuses it instead of generating a new ID.

The test scenario is: session continues without a new prompt submit (user didn't enter a new prompt but the agent did more work). In this case, we DO need a new checkpoint ID because there IS new content.

Looking at the `PrepareCommitMsg` flow, when `hasNewContent` is true, it first checks `PendingCheckpointID` from sessions with content, and if found, reuses it. The `PendingCheckpointID` is supposed to be for amend restoration — but it's also used for idempotency across hook re-invocations.

The issue is: `PendingCheckpointID` is cleared by `InitializeSession` when a new prompt starts (lines 1027-1032), but this test deliberately skips `SimulateUserPromptSubmit` between commits. So the old `PendingCheckpointID` persists.

The right fix is more subtle: `PendingCheckpointID` should be cleared after it has been **consumed** by condensation, not by SaveChanges. Let me re-examine what `condenseAndUpdateState` does:

Looking at `condenseAndUpdateState` (line ~720-731):
```go
state.LastCheckpointID = checkpointID
state.PendingCheckpointID = checkpointID.String()
```

So `condenseAndUpdateState` sets `PendingCheckpointID` to the checkpoint ID that was just condensed. This is for amend restoration. After condensation, the next PrepareCommitMsg that finds new content should NOT reuse this PendingCheckpointID — it should generate a new one.

But the problem is timing. The `PendingCheckpointID` is used in PrepareCommitMsg to:
1. Allow amend to restore a trailer 
2. Ensure idempotency if PrepareCommitMsg runs multiple times for the same commit

The key insight: `PendingCheckpointID` should be consumed by the NEXT commit (for idempotency/amend) but after that commit's condensation, it should point to the NEW checkpoint, not persist forever.

Actually, looking more carefully at the code: after the first commit's condensation, both `LastCheckpointID` and `PendingCheckpointID` get set. When the second commit's `PrepareCommitMsg` runs with new content, it iterates `sessionsWithContent` and finds `PendingCheckpointID` is non-empty, so it reuses that ID. This is wrong because the PendingCheckpointID was from the PREVIOUS commit.

The issue is that `PendingCheckpointID` serves two purposes:
1. **Amend trailer restoration** (handle-amend-commit-msg)
2. **Idempotency for PrepareCommitMsg** (avoid generating different IDs on multiple hook invocations for the same commit)

My Bug 3 fix (not clearing in SaveChanges) is correct — SaveChanges shouldn't touch it. But the _real_ clearing should happen at a different point. Looking at the existing code, `InitializeSession` clears both IDs on every new prompt. The test case that fails is specifically when there's NO new prompt between commits.

The correct place to clear `PendingCheckpointID` is **after condensation succeeds** — but only set it to the new value (which is what `condenseAndUpdateState` already does). The problem is that for the ACTIVE → ACTIVE_COMMITTED flow, `PostCommit` sets `PendingCheckpointID = checkpointID.String()` as part of `ActionMigrateShadowBranch` (line ~638). Then when condensation runs at TurnEnd, it reads and uses that ID. After condensation, `condenseAndUpdateState` overwrites it with the condensed ID.

For the case without `SimulateUserPromptSubmit`: after first condensation, `PendingCheckpointID = "af924955f841"`. Then new work happens, SaveChanges runs (no longer clears it), then second commit's PrepareCommitMsg finds it and reuses it.

The fix should be: clear `PendingCheckpointID` in the `PostCommit` handler when condensation completes **for the case where the session is IDLE** (immediate condensation). After successful condensation in PostCommit, we want `LastCheckpointID` to persist (for reuse in subsequent commits), but `PendingCheckpointID` should only persist for amend restoration (and the amend handler checks it). Actually, looking at `condenseAndUpdateState`, it sets **both** — so `PendingCheckpointID` persists intentionally for amend.

The real issue is: `PrepareCommitMsg` uses `PendingCheckpointID` as a signal for "this session already has a checkpoint ID being prepared" when it should only be used for amend. Let me re-read the comment:

```go
// New content: check PendingCheckpointID first (set during previous condensation),
// otherwise generate a new one. This ensures idempotent IDs across hook invocations.
```

The "idempotent IDs across hook invocations" comment refers to the case where `prepare-commit-msg` is called multiple times for the same commit (git sometimes does this). But after a successful condensation, the next commit should get a NEW ID.

The cleanest fix: after condensation succeeds in `condenseAndUpdateState`, clear `PendingCheckpointID` but keep `LastCheckpointID`. `PendingCheckpointID` was originally meant for the ACTIVE_COMMITTED → TurnEnd deferred condensation flow (where PostCommit sets it and TurnEnd consumes it). After condensation, it shouldn't linger.

Wait, but the amend flow in `handleAmendCommitMsg` checks `PendingCheckpointID` too. Let me reconsider...

The amend flow needs `PendingCheckpointID` to restore a trailer when the user does `git commit --amend -m "new msg"`. The `PendingCheckpointID` is set during condensation so that if the user amends the commit, the trailer can be restored. But `LastCheckpointID` also exists for this purpose (it's used in the non-amend PrepareCommitMsg path for reusing IDs on subsequent commits).

Looking at `handleAmendCommitMsg`: it checks `PendingCheckpointID` only. Looking at the non-amend path: it checks `PendingCheckpointID` when `hasNewContent` is true (for idempotency). 

I think the right approach is: `PendingCheckpointID` should NOT be used in the `hasNewContent` PrepareCommitMsg path at all — it should only be used for amend restoration. The idempotency concern can be handled differently (or isn't actually needed because PrepareCommitMsg generates a new ID each time it's called, which is fine).

Actually wait, let me re-read the PrepareCommitMsg code more carefully:

```go
if hasNewContent {
    // New content: check PendingCheckpointID first (set during previous condensation),
    // otherwise generate a new one. This ensures idempotent IDs across hook invocations.
    for _, state := range sessionsWithContent {
        if state.PendingCheckpointID != "" {
            if cpID, err := id.NewCheckpointID(state.PendingCheckpointID); err == nil {
                checkpointID = cpID
                break
            }
        }
    }
```

This code checks `PendingCheckpointID` in `sessionsWithContent` — these are sessions that have NEW content. If PendingCheckpointID was set during a PREVIOUS condensation, it shouldn't be reused for new content.

The fix for this integration test is: `condenseAndUpdateState` should NOT set `PendingCheckpointID`. It was only needed for the deferred condensation flow (ACTIVE → ACTIVE_COMMITTED → TurnEnd), where PostCommit sets it and TurnEnd consumes it. After condensation (whether immediate or deferred), `PendingCheckpointID` should be cleared.

Let me re-examine the flow:
1. PostCommit (ACTIVE → ACTIVE_COMMITTED): sets `state.PendingCheckpointID = checkpointID.String()` — this is correct, it's consumed by TurnEnd's handleTurnEndCondense.
2. `condenseAndUpdateState`: sets `state.PendingCheckpointID = checkpointID.String()` — this is for amend restoration.
3. `handleAmendCommitMsg`: reads `PendingCheckpointID` to restore trailer on amend.

For amend restoration, `LastCheckpointID` would work equally well. Let me check if `handleAmendCommitMsg` can use `LastCheckpointID` instead... it currently only checks `PendingCheckpointID`. But `LastCheckpointID` is also set by `condenseAndUpdateState`.

The cleanest fix: in `condenseAndUpdateState`, don't set `PendingCheckpointID`. Let the amend handler use `LastCheckpointID` instead. And update `handleAmendCommitMsg` to also check `LastCheckpointID`.

Actually, let me look more carefully at what `PendingCheckpointID` is used for vs `LastCheckpointID`:
- `PendingCheckpointID` (string): Set by PostCommit for deferred condensation; set by condenseAndUpdateState for amend; read by handleAmendCommitMsg; read by PrepareCommitMsg (hasNewContent path for "idempotency")
- `LastCheckpointID` (id.CheckpointID): Set by condenseAndUpdateState; read by PrepareCommitMsg (no-new-content path for checkpoint ID reuse)

The simplest fix: stop setting `PendingCheckpointID` in `condenseAndUpdateState`, and update `handleAmendCommitMsg` to fall back to `LastCheckpointID` as well.

   435→// handleAmendCommitMsg handles the prepare-commit-msg hook for amend operations
   436→// (source="commit"). It preserves existing trailers or restores from PendingCheckpointID.
   437→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) handleAmendCommitMsg(logCtx context.Context, commitMsgFile string) error {
   438→	// Read current commit message
   439→	content, err := os.ReadFile(commitMsgFile) //nolint:gosec // commitMsgFile is provided by git hook
   440→	if err != nil {
   441→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   442→	}
   443→
   444→	message := string(content)
   445→
   446→	// If message already has a trailer, keep it unchanged
   447→	if existingCpID, found := trailers.ParseCheckpoint(message); found {
   448→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: amend preserves existing trailer",
   449→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   450→			slog.String("checkpoint_id", existingCpID.String()),
   451→		)
   452→		return nil
   453→	}
   454→
   455→	// No trailer in message — check if any session has PendingCheckpointID to restore
   456→	worktreePath, err := GetWorktreePath()
   457→	if err != nil {
   458→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   459→	}
   460→
   461→	sessions, err := s.findSessionsForWorktree(worktreePath)
   462→	if err != nil || len(sessions) == 0 {
   463→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // No sessions - nothing to restore
   464→	}
   465→
   466→	// Find first session with PendingCheckpointID
   467→	for _, state := range sessions {
   468→		if state.PendingCheckpointID == "" {
   469→			continue
   470→		}
   471→
   472→		cpID, err := id.NewCheckpointID(state.PendingCheckpointID)
   473→		if err != nil {
   474→			continue // Invalid ID, skip
   475→		}
   476→
   477→		// Restore the trailer
   478→		message = addCheckpointTrailer(message, cpID)
   479→		if writeErr := os.WriteFile(commitMsgFile, []byte(message), 0o600); writeErr != nil {
   480→			return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   481→		}
   482→
   483→		logging.Info(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: restored trailer on amend from PendingCheckpointID",
   484→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   485→			slog.String("checkpoint_id", cpID.String()),
   486→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   487→		)
   488→		return nil
   489→	}
   490→
   491→	// No PendingCheckpointID found - leave message unchanged
   492→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: amend with no pending checkpoint to restore",
   493→		slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   494→	)
   495→	return nil
   496→}
   497→
   498→// PostCommit is called by the git post-commit hook after a commit is created.
   499→// Uses the session state machine to determine what action to take per session:
   500→//   - ACTIVE → ACTIVE_COMMITTED: defer condensation (agent still working)
   501→//   - IDLE → condense immediately
   502→//   - ACTIVE_COMMITTED → migrate shadow branch (additional commit during same turn)
   503→//   - ENDED → condense if files touched, discard if empty
   504→//

> AGENT

Now I have a clear picture. Looking at the ACTIVE → GitCommit transition (line 200-206): it goes to `ACTIVE_COMMITTED` and emits `ActionMigrateShadowBranch`. In the handler (line 637), `PendingCheckpointID` is set for migration. This is the "deferred condensation" flow — condensation happens later at TurnEnd.

The key issue was: `condenseAndUpdateState` used to set `PendingCheckpointID = checkpointID.String()` which caused the integration test to reuse the same checkpoint ID for the next commit. Now it clears it.

The `handleAmendCommitMsg` needs to check `LastCheckpointID` as fallback. Let me update it:

> TOOL

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/alex/workspace/cli/.worktrees/1/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go",
  "old_string": "\t// Find first session with PendingCheckpointID\n\tfor _, state := range sessions {\n\t\tif state.PendingCheckpointID == \"\" {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tcpID, err := id.NewCheckpointID(state.PendingCheckpointID)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tcontinue // Invalid ID, skip\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Restore the trailer\n\t\tmessage = addCheckpointTrailer(message, cpID)\n\t\tif writeErr := os.WriteFile(commitMsgFile, []byte(message), 0o600); writeErr != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tlogging.Info(logCtx, \"prepare-commit-msg: restored trailer on amend from PendingCheckpointID\",\n\t\t\tslog.String(\"strategy\", \"manual-commit\"),\n\t\t\tslog.String(\"checkpoint_id\", cpID.String()),\n\t\t\tslog.String(\"session_id\", state.SessionID),\n\t\t)\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\t// No PendingCheckpointID found - leave message unchanged\n\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"prepare-commit-msg: amend with no pending checkpoint to restore\",\n\t\tslog.String(\"strategy\", \"manual-commit\"),\n\t)",
  "new_string": "\t// Find first session with PendingCheckpointID or LastCheckpointID to restore.\n\t// PendingCheckpointID is set during ACTIVE_COMMITTED (deferred condensation).\n\t// LastCheckpointID is set after condensation completes.\n\tfor _, state := range sessions {\n\t\tvar cpID id.CheckpointID\n\t\tsource := \"\"\n\n\t\tif state.PendingCheckpointID != \"\" {\n\t\t\tif parsed, parseErr := id.NewCheckpointID(state.PendingCheckpointID); parseErr == nil {\n\t\t\t\tcpID = parsed\n\t\t\t\tsource = \"PendingCheckpointID\"\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t\tif cpID.IsEmpty() && !state.LastCheckpointID.IsEmpty() {\n\t\t\tcpID = state.LastCheckpointID\n\t\t\tsource = \"LastCheckpointID\"\n\t\t}\n\t\tif cpID.IsEmpty() {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Restore the trailer\n\t\tmessage = addCheckpointTrailer(message, cpID)\n\t\tif writeErr := os.WriteFile(commitMsgFile, []byte(message), 0o600); writeErr != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tlogging.Info(logCtx, \"prepare-commit-msg: restored trailer on amend\",\n\t\t\tslog.String(\"strategy\", \"manual-commit\"),\n\t\t\tslog.String(\"checkpoint_id\", cpID.String()),\n\t\t\tslog.String(\"session_id\", state.SessionID),\n\t\t\tslog.String(\"source\", source),\n\t\t)\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\t// No checkpoint ID found - leave message unchanged\n\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"prepare-commit-msg: amend with no checkpoint to restore\",\n\t\tslog.String(\"strategy\", \"manual-commit\"),\n\t)"

Now let me also check if `PrepareCommitMsg`'s normal path (non-amend) has the same issue with `PendingCheckpointID` reuse. Looking at lines 332-342, when `hasNewContent` is true, it checks `PendingCheckpointID` first. Since we now clear it in `condenseAndUpdateState`, this should be fine — after a condensation, `PendingCheckpointID` is empty, so the next commit with new content will generate a fresh ID.

But wait — let me also check that `PrepareCommitMsg`'s `isRestoringExisting` check (line 382-387) still works correctly. If `PendingCheckpointID` is empty, `isRestoringExisting` will be `false`, which means the user gets the confirmation prompt for new content. That's correct — after condensation, the next commit genuinely has new content and should get a new ID with confirmation.

> AGENT

All five steps are complete. Here's a summary of the changes made:

## Summary: ENT-258 Mid-turn commits fix

### Bug 1 (Path mismatch) — `manual_commit_hooks.go`
In `sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript`, transcript paths are absolute (`/Users/alex/project/src/main.go`) but staged files are repo-relative (`src/main.go`). Added normalization using `GetWorktreePath()` + `paths.ToRelativePath()` before the overlap comparison.

### Bug 2 (BaseCommit not updated) — `manual_commit_hooks.go`
`PostCommit` early-returned when no trailer was found. Added `postCommitUpdateBaseCommitOnly()` helper that finds worktree sessions and updates their `BaseCommit` to new HEAD without triggering state machine transitions.

### Bug 3 (PendingCheckpointID cleared) — `manual_commit_git.go`
Removed the `state.PendingCheckpointID = ""` clearing in `SaveChanges`. Added comment explaining it's intentionally preserved for deferred condensation.

### Follow-on fix (checkpoint ID reuse) — `manual_commit_hooks.go`
- `condenseAndUpdateState` now clears `PendingCheckpointID` after condensation (was setting it, causing reuse across commits)
- `handleAmendCommitMsg` now falls back to `LastCheckpointID` when `PendingCheckpointID` is empty

### Tests — `mid_turn_commit_test.go` (new file)
Three targeted unit tests that verify each bug fix.

> AGENT

That's the problem. `t.Chdir` changes the process-wide working directory, which means these tests **cannot** run in parallel — they'd clobber each other's cwd. `t.Chdir` (introduced in Go 1.24) does set up cleanup to restore the original cwd, but concurrent tests would still race on the global cwd state.

So the lack of `t.Parallel()` is intentional because of `t.Chdir`. The strategy tests rely on `os.Getwd()` internally (via `GetWorktreePath()`, `OpenRepository()`, etc.), so they must run sequentially within the package.

The ~14s runtime for 177 tests running sequentially is actually not terrible — it was likely always this way. The 3 new tests I added aren't the cause. But let me double-check what the runtime was before my changes:

> AGENT

The 3 new tests add ~0.24s total — negligible.

**The slow strategy tests are a pre-existing issue**, not something we introduced. The reason: every test uses `t.Chdir()` which mutates the process-wide working directory, making `t.Parallel()` impossible. The strategy code relies on `os.Getwd()` via `GetWorktreePath()`, `OpenRepository()`, etc.

To actually fix the slowness would require refactoring the strategy code to accept explicit paths instead of reading `os.Getwd()` — that's a much larger change and unrelated to the ENT-258 work. Want me to file a ticket for that?

 	}
 }
 
+// postCommitUpdateBaseCommitOnly updates BaseCommit for all sessions on the current
+// worktree when a commit has no Entire-Checkpoint trailer. This prevents BaseCommit
+// from going stale, which would cause future PrepareCommitMsg calls to skip the
+// session (BaseCommit != currentHeadHash filter).
+//
+// Unlike the full PostCommit flow, this does NOT fire EventGitCommit or trigger
+// condensation — it only keeps BaseCommit in sync with HEAD.
+func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) postCommitUpdateBaseCommitOnly(logCtx context.Context, head *plumbing.Reference) {
+	worktreePath, err := GetWorktreePath()
+	if err != nil {
+		return // Silent failure — hooks must be resilient
+	}
+
+	sessions, err := s.findSessionsForWorktree(worktreePath)
+	if err != nil || len(sessions) == 0 {
+		return
+	}
+
+	newHead := head.Hash().String()
+	for _, state := range sessions {
+		if state.BaseCommit != newHead {
+			logging.Debug(logCtx, "post-commit (no trailer): updating BaseCommit",
+				slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
+				slog.String("old_base", state.BaseCommit[:7]),
+				slog.String("new_head", newHead[:7]),
+			)
+			state.BaseCommit = newHead
+			if err := s.saveSessionState(state); err != nil {
+				fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[entire] Warning: failed to update session state: %v\n", err)
+			}
+		}
+	}
+}
+

 // beyond what was already condensed.

+	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
+
 	// Need both transcript path and agent type to analyze
 	if state.TranscriptPath == "" || state.AgentType == "" {
+		logging.Debug(logCtx, "live transcript check: missing transcript path or agent type",
+			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
+			slog.String("transcript_path", state.TranscriptPath),
+			slog.String("agent_type", string(state.AgentType)),
+		)
 		return false, nil
 	}

 	// Check if transcript has grown since last condensation

+			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),

 	}
 
@@ -880,14 +938,49 @@ func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript(repo *git.
 
 	// No file modifications means no new content to checkpoint
 	if len(modifiedFiles) == 0 {
+		logging.Debug(logCtx, "live transcript check: transcript grew but no file modifications",
+			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
+		)
 		return false, nil
 	}
 
+	// Normalize modified files from absolute to repo-relative paths.
+	// Transcript tool_use entries contain absolute paths (e.g., /Users/alex/project/src/main.go)
+	// but getStagedFiles returns repo-relative paths (e.g., src/main.go).
+	worktreePath, wpErr := GetWorktreePath()
+	if wpErr == nil {
+		normalized := make([]string, 0, len(modifiedFiles))
+		for _, f := range modifiedFiles {
+			if rel := paths.ToRelativePath(f, worktreePath); rel != "" {
+				normalized = append(normalized, rel)
+			} else {
+				// Already relative or outside repo — keep as-is
+				normalized = append(normalized, f)
+			}
+		}
+		modifiedFiles = normalized
+	}
+
+	logging.Debug(logCtx, "live transcript check: found file modifications",
+		slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
+		slog.Int("modified_files", len(modifiedFiles)),
+	)
+
 	// Check if any modified files overlap with currently staged files
 	// This ensures we only add checkpoint trailers to commits that include
 	// files the agent actually modified
-	stagedFiles := getStagedFiles(repo) // TODO: does it work? Do we have staged files if this is called at post-commit hook ?
+	stagedFiles := getStagedFiles(repo)
+
+	logging.Debug(logCtx, "live transcript check: comparing staged vs modified",
+		slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
+		slog.Int("staged_files", len(stagedFiles)),
+		slog.Int("modified_files", len(modifiedFiles)),
+	)
+
 	if !hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, modifiedFiles) {
+		logging.Debug(logCtx, "live transcript check: no overlap between staged and modified files",
+			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
+		)
 		return false, nil // No overlap - staged files are unrelated to agent's work
 	}

Added normalization via `GetWorktreePath()` + `paths.ToRelativePath()`.\n- **BaseCommit staleness**: `PostCommit` early-returned when no trailer was found, never updating `BaseCommit`. Subsequent commits failed the `BaseCommit == currentHeadHash` filter in `PrepareCommitMsg`. Added `postCommitUpdateBaseCommitOnly()` to keep BaseCommit in sync.\n- **PendingCheckpointID cleared prematurely**: `SaveChanges` cleared `PendingCheckpointID` which was needed for deferred condensation at turn end. Removed the clearing; condensation now clears it after use instead.\n\n## Test plan\n\n- [x] Unit tests for all three bugs (`mid_turn_commit_test.go`)\n- [x] `mise run fmt && mise run lint` clean\n- [x] `mise run test:ci` passes (unit + integration)\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1",
  "description": "Create draft PR"
}

   161→// and should persist through SaveChanges calls until consumed by handleTurnEndCondense.
   162→//
   163→// Bug: SaveChanges clears PendingCheckpointID at line ~120. When the agent stops,
   164→// handleTurnEndCondense finds it empty, generates a new ID, and the commit trailer
   165→// and condensed data end up with different IDs.
   166→func TestSaveChanges_PreservesPendingCheckpointID(t *testing.T) {
   167→	dir := setupGitRepo(t)
   168→	t.Chdir(dir)
   169→
   170→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(dir)
   171→	require.NoError(t, err)
   172→
   173→	s := &ManualCommitStrategy{}
   174→	sessionID := "test-preserve-pending-cpid"
   175→
   176→	// Initialize session and save first checkpoint
   177→	setupSessionWithCheckpoint(t, s, repo, dir, sessionID)
   178→
   179→	// Set PendingCheckpointID (simulating what PostCommit does)
   180→	state, err := s.loadSessionState(sessionID)
   181→	require.NoError(t, err)
   182→	state.PendingCheckpointID = "abc123def456"
   183→	state.Phase = session.PhaseActiveCommitted
   184→	require.NoError(t, s.saveSessionState(state))
   185→
   186→	// Create metadata for a new checkpoint
   187→	metadataDir := ".entire/metadata/" + sessionID
   188→	metadataDirAbs := filepath.Join(dir, metadataDir)
   189→	// Transcript already exists from setupSessionWithCheckpoint
   190→
   191→	// Modify a file so the checkpoint has real changes
   192→	testFile := filepath.Join(dir, "src", "new_file.go")
   193→	require.NoError(t, os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(testFile), 0o755))
   194→	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(testFile, []byte("package src\n"), 0o644))
   195→
   196→	// Call SaveChanges — this should NOT clear PendingCheckpointID
   197→	err = s.SaveChanges(SaveContext{
   198→		SessionID:      sessionID,
   199→		ModifiedFiles:  []string{},
   200→		NewFiles:       []string{"src/new_file.go"},
   201→		DeletedFiles:   []string{},
   202→		MetadataDir:    metadataDir,
   203→		MetadataDirAbs: metadataDirAbs,
   204→		CommitMessage:  "Checkpoint 2",
   205→		AuthorName:     "Test",
   206→		AuthorEmail:    "test@test.com",
   207→	})
   208→	require.NoError(t, err)
   209→
   210→	// Reload state and verify PendingCheckpointID is preserved
   211→	state, err = s.loadSessionState(sessionID)
   212→	require.NoError(t, err)
   213→	assert.Equal(t, "abc123def456", state.PendingCheckpointID,
   214→		"PendingCheckpointID should be preserved across SaveChanges calls, "+
   215→			"not cleared — it's needed for deferred condensation at turn end")
   216→}

> AGENT

Both are valid points. Let me look at the current code for each.

**Comment 1**: Use `state.WorktreePath` instead of calling `GetWorktreePath()` — good, avoids a subprocess.

**Comment 2**: `handleAmendCommitMsg` LastCheckpointID fallback is too broad — could attach a stale checkpoint ID from an old session. Should filter by `BaseCommit == HEAD` or active phase.

> AGENT

OK, now I have the full picture. Here's what I see:

**`PendingCheckpointID`** (string) — the checkpoint ID from a **commit trailer** that needs to be reused in deferred condensation:
- **Set by**: PostCommit `ActionMigrateShadowBranch` (ACTIVE → ACTIVE_COMMITTED, line 665)
- **Consumed by**: `handleTurnEndCondense` (line 1410) — uses it instead of generating a new ID
- **Also checked by**: `PrepareCommitMsg` normal path (line 336) to reuse ID, and `handleAmendCommitMsg` (line 490)
- **Cleared by**: `condenseAndUpdateState` (line 760), `HandleTurnStart` (line 1159), `CondenseSessionByID` (bug — still sets it)

**`LastCheckpointID`** (id.CheckpointID) — the checkpoint ID from the **most recent condensation**, used to reuse the same ID on subsequent commits without new content:
- **Set by**: `condenseAndUpdateState` (line 756), `CondenseSessionByID` (line 568)
- **Consumed by**: `PrepareCommitMsg` no-new-content path (line 294) — reuses existing ID
- **Also checked by**: `handleAmendCommitMsg` (line 496) as fallback, session cleanup (line 90)
- **Cleared by**: `HandleTurnStart` (line 1156)

To your question — **do we need both?** Looking at this carefully:

The only place `PendingCheckpointID` is meaningfully distinct from `LastCheckpointID` is in the **ACTIVE_COMMITTED deferred condensation flow**: PostCommit sets it from the commit trailer (line 665), and later `handleTurnEndCondense` (line 1410) reads it to match the already-committed trailer. At that point, `LastCheckpointID` hasn't been updated yet (condensation hasn't run), so `PendingCheckpointID` is the only place the ID lives.

After condensation, `LastCheckpointID` gets the same value and `PendingCheckpointID` is cleared. So post-condensation they converge.

**We could unify them** — `LastCheckpointID` could serve both purposes if we set it eagerly in PostCommit's `ActionMigrateShadowBranch` instead of only after condensation. But that would change its semantics: currently `LastCheckpointID` means "the ID from the last completed condensation" (verified data), whereas `PendingCheckpointID` means "the ID we expect to use for a not-yet-completed condensation" (promise). If condensation fails, the distinction matters — `LastCheckpointID` stays empty (correct, no data), while `PendingCheckpointID` had the trailer ID (which is now orphaned).

That said, this is a pretty edge-case distinction. In practice, condensation almost never fails. I'd say: keep them separate for now since merging has subtle implications, but it's a reasonable cleanup for a follow-up. The more pressing thing is fixing the inconsistency in `CondenseSessionByID`.

Let me fix that now:

> AGENT

Done. To answer your question about **whether we need both `PendingCheckpointID` and `LastCheckpointID`**:

**Short answer**: Yes, for now — they serve different lifecycle purposes.

- **`PendingCheckpointID`** = "the ID from a commit trailer that hasn't been condensed yet" — set in PostCommit (ACTIVE → ACTIVE_COMMITTED), consumed by `handleTurnEndCondense` at TurnEnd. It's a *promise* that condensation will use this specific ID.

- **`LastCheckpointID`** = "the ID from the last completed condensation" — set after condensation succeeds, used by PrepareCommitMsg to reuse the same ID on subsequent no-new-content commits.

The critical distinction: if condensation **fails**, `PendingCheckpointID` held the trailer ID (now orphaned), but `LastCheckpointID` correctly remains empty (no data was committed). Merging them would mean setting `LastCheckpointID` eagerly before condensation, which would falsely claim data exists when it doesn't.

That said, condensation failure is extremely rare in practice. Unifying into just `LastCheckpointID` (set eagerly, cleared on failure) is a reasonable simplification for a follow-up — just not worth doing in this bug-fix PR.

> DEVELOPER
